6c

Case simplicity

7 answers
Judgement60C-7930D-0264D-0358D-0462D-0547D-0650
Group judgement

Risks

C+609th of 35

Layer 3 · Risks

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The financial forecasts for the next 18 months are predictable in their profit, less predictable in their composition, and unpredictable in the multiple that will be paid for them. The two earlier sets agree on the case and their disagreements have been settled by evidence in the second set's favour (D-01). The thesis rests about three-quarters on strengths Carlsberg has demonstrated repeatedly (guidance beaten every year since 2016, synergies ahead of plan, cost flexed to protect profit in 2020, 2022-23 and 2025, deleveraging ahead of schedule, PepsiCo's repeated choice) and about one-quarter on a Kazakhstan ramp at the fast end of precedent and on pricing holding in contested markets (D-02). The drivers that separate the next four years from the last six are concentrated and mostly mechanical: the end of three external drags, dated synergies, the balance-sheet leg, and one diffuse driver, price over cost, that decides FY2028-30 (D-03). The cone is about ±5% on FY2027 EPS and ±8% on FY2028, slightly skewed down in 2027 and neutral in 2028, with the risk front-loaded into H1 2027 and the positives back-loaded into H2 (D-04). Disclosure lets the Consumer monitor the profit floor and the pricing test but not China's profit, the Kazakhstan margin or the EPS composition until February 2027 (D-05). The trace from management's commitments to EBIT is clear and the trace from EBIT to EPS is where clarity is lost, in four unguided lines that the sell-side models inconsistently (D-06). The multiple is set about two-thirds by a sector at decade-low ratings and one-third by a company-specific discount that only the buyback and China can close (D-07).

The steer. For an 18-month investment case, predictability of the earnings is a strength: FY2026 is nearly done, FY2027 EBIT is bounded by a management that manages to its number, and the items that can surprise are few, dated and observable. The weaknesses are the pinch-year composition (the hybrid, rising minorities, a buyback that starts late), which will reshuffle the sell-side's bridges in February 2027, and a multiple that the company does not control. The model, corrected for minorities, gives EPS of DKK 66.3, 70.0, 76.4 and 82.5 for FY2026-29; the memo should rest on those, hold the FY2027 range at DKK 66.5-72.5, and separate the return the company can deliver from the return the sector will decide.

C-79

How narrow is the business perimeter (number of countries and business lines), how is it changing, and what does that mean for predictability?

F3030th of 35

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement. The perimeter is wide, and widening in category:

  • a commercial presence in more than 125 markets;
  • production in about 30 countries;
  • three reported regions;
  • two categories: beer 67% and soft drinks and other 33% of volume;
  • 17 PepsiCo markets by 2029.

It is narrowing in geography: Russia is gone and 25% of Southeast Asia is sold. Profit is predictable: the guidance culture and diversification smooth it. Volume is not, because of China and weather.

What breadth means for the case. It makes the case hard to track and, in the Fitness for Fighting digest's terms, fragile: that digest gives cases with many moving parts its Fragility Double Underweight.

For the model. Build by region, with China split inside Asia and India and Kazakhstan split inside CEEI, and by category. Model associates (Super Bock, the Chinese associates) separately below operating profit.

D-02

To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and to what extent does the thesis rely only on strengths the business demonstrably has, rather than on strengths it would need to acquire?

D6423rd of 35

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. Over the 18 months to mid-2028, the thesis rests about three-quarters on demonstrated strengths and one-quarter on strengths that are being acquired but are not yet proven. Beyond 2027 the balance shifts: FY2028-30 EBIT growth of about 5% a year needs price to run ahead of cost without a named cost programme, which Carlsberg has done once (2019) and only with a Chinese growth engine it no longer has. The expected change is therefore plausible for FY2026-27 on the record alone, and plausible for FY2028-30 only if one of two things appears: a new efficiency programme (likely at a new strategy cycle by 2028) or real pricing in the challenger markets (not demonstrated). The forecasts in our base need neither for the next 18 months, which is why they carry weight; the memo's three-year leg needs one of them.

The demonstrated strengths, and the forecast line each one carries:

Strength Evidence it is demonstrated What in our base rests on it
Delivering profit guidance at or above the top of the initial range Every year since 2016 (beats of 300-870bp; 2022: 12.2% against 0-7%; 2024: 6.0% against 1-5%; 2025: 5.0% against 1-5%); upward narrowing at the half year in 2023, 2024, 2025 and 2026 FY2026 organic EBIT +5.75%; FY2027 +5.0% with a slight upward lean
Protecting absolute profit through cost spikes by flexing operating expenses and accepting a lower gross margin 2011: cost of sales per hectolitre +8%, price and mix +4%; 2020: revenue -8.4% organically, EBIT -3.1%, marketing and administration cut 14%; 2022: cost of sales per hectolitre +13%, gross margin -190bp, profit protected; 2023: organic EBIT +5.2% with gross margin -100bp; 2025: volume -2.0%, organic EBIT +5.0% The 2027 cost year: gross margin flat to -30bp, EBIT +5%, on a 160bp input headwind (Morgan Stanley)
Integrating fast and over-delivering on synergies Funding the Journey: DKK 3bn against a DKK 1.5-2.0bn target (2015-18); Britvic: about 30% of synergies in year one against a 10-15% plan, target raised to GBP 110m, about 80% by end-2026 DKK 475m of synergies in FY2026 and DKK 190m in FY2027
Deleveraging on or ahead of schedule, then restarting buybacks 2.34x (2015) to 1.25x (2019), buybacks restarted in 2017 at 1.45x against a 2.0x ceiling, DKK 4.1bn bought back in 2019 (41% of free cash flow); 3.9x (H1 2025) to 3.0x (H1 2026), about 2.5x at end-2026 a year early Leverage about 2.5x at end-2026; DKK 2bn buyback in H2 2027, DKK 4-5bn a year from 2028
Being chosen repeatedly by PepsiCo, and making a licence pay in a market it already leads Norway 1998: Pepsi share from 10% to 33% on the beer route to market; 5 PepsiCo markets in 2024 to 17 by 2029; Pepsi volume share in the UK up more than 1 point in Q1 2026 Kazakhstan +DKK 200m EBIT in 2027 and +DKK 100-150m in 2028; Azerbaijan breakeven in 2027, +DKK 50-80m in 2028
Absorbing a licence change San Miguel (UK, January 2025): Poretti's volume more than doubled, organic revenue excluding San Miguel +1.1% in a -0.6% year; Coca-Cola border trade (2020): Danish canning-line volume -30%, absorbed within Western Europe The 2029 Nordic swap modelled as a one-off DKK 150m EBIT cost and a net 1.5m hectolitre loss, not as a structural break

The strengths that would need to be acquired, and the forecast line each one threatens:

  1. Real pricing above inflation in markets where Carlsberg is a challenger (66% of its volume). The record shows nominal pricing: excise and cost pass-through with a lag, not price above inflation. The 2011 precedent cost 250bp of volume share in a quarter; 2024's above-market pricing in France cost off-trade share; the UK mainstream brand lost on-trade share to Heineken's and Molson Coors' alcohol-strength price cuts in 2026. Our base does not need real pricing in 2027 (pure price 2.6% against local inflation of 2-3%, covering 85-95% of input inflation in money). It does need price to hold at about 2.5% a year in 2028-30 while input inflation moderates, which is the industry norm rather than a Carlsberg-specific strength. The line at risk is 2027 gross margin, by about 30-60bp (DKK 300-450m of EBIT, 2-3%) if price and mix fall below 2%.
  2. Premium beer execution in Asia against Heineken. Heineken's licensed volume through China Resources Beer grew in the mid-twenties in Q3 2026 while Carlsberg's China volume fell about 10%; 1664 Blanc fell 5%; local brands fell 11% in Q3 (Jefferies). The Carlsberg brand grew 20% in China in Q3, so the equity is not broken, but the super-premium and mainstream engines are. Our base needs only comparatives from China in 2027 (volume +2%, EBIT +3-5%), not execution. The line at risk is Asia's margin in 2027 (Rothschild & Co Redburn has China's operating margin falling a further 100bp to 21% in 2027, against our flat-to-up Asia margin), worth about DKK 100-150m of EBIT, half of it reaching EPS after minorities.
  3. Turning a toll-filled soft-drink business into one near group margins within a year. Peers took two to three years (Royal Unibrew's PepsiCo ramp 2025-27; Coca-Cola Europacific Partners' German turnaround; Coca-Cola HBC's Egyptian route-to-market overhaul). Our DKK 200m step in 2027 is about the annualised removal of the H1 2026 toll-filling drag (40bp of CEEI margin, about DKK 160m a year) plus modest utilisation gains on Rothschild & Co Redburn's 35% gross margin on DKK 1,450m of sales. That is at the fast end of the precedents but not beyond them, because the plant replaces an identified cost rather than needing new demand. Kazakhstan's 11% inflation and rising utility tariffs are the risk; the line at risk is about DKK 50-100m of CEEI EBIT in 2027.
  4. Growing revenue at 4-6% a year. Never delivered since the target was set (2024: 2.4%; 2025: -0.6%; H1 2026: 2.7%; Q3 2026: 3.0%); the incentive plan's revenue element paid 12.10 of 20.00. Our base does not use it (2.9-3.2% organic). It threatens nothing in our forecasts and everything in management's informal "high-single-digit operating profit compounding", which we hold as the upside case.
  5. Sustaining mid-single-digit EBIT growth after the cost programmes end. The precedent is 2019: after Funding the Journey closed in 2018, organic EBIT grew 10.5% on revenue of 3.2% with price and mix of 3% and Asian volume growth, and Jefferies noted the cost culture "remained deeply embedded". The peers' precedents are worse: Heineken's EverGreen savings of more than EUR 3bn since 2019 dropped through at only about 25%; Diageo's USD 540m of savings in fiscal 2026 were consumed by volume declines; Molson Coors' programmes fund marketing rather than margin. Carlsberg's 2019 was achieved with China growing double digits in profit. Without that engine, FY2028-30 EBIT growth of 5% needs either about 1-2 points a year of price over cost (plausible if 2027's inflation moderates) or a new programme. We hold 5% because management's culture is demonstrated and the Nordic model's density gains are mechanical, but we weight FY2028-30 at only 40-50%, as the second set did.

The arithmetic of reliance for FY2027. Our FY2027 EBIT increase of DKK 728m decomposes as synergies +190 (demonstrated), Kazakhstan +200 (being acquired, fast end of precedent), China +100 (comparatives, mechanical), India +90 (demonstrated mid-teens volume growth; Karnataka's excise reform helps), and underlying price over cost net of the marketing step-up +150 (demonstrated at the absolute-profit level, not at the margin level). About DKK 530m of the DKK 728m (73%) rests on demonstrated strengths or mechanics; about DKK 200m (27%) on the Kazakhstan ramp and on pricing holding in contested markets. Below EBIT, the buyback restart rests on a demonstrated habit; the finance-cost saving rests on arithmetic already visible in the H1 2026 bond redemptions and on a Sapporo closing that management confirmed for Q4 2026.

What would make the change implausible. Three observations within 18 months would tell the memo Analyst that the thesis has started to rely on acquired strengths: Q1 2027 revenue per hectolitre below +2% (price not covering costs); CEEI's H1 2027 margin below 18% (Kazakhstan not stepping); Chongqing Brewery's H1 2027 operating margin still falling year on year with inventories normal (China structural, not weather). None of these breaks the FY2026 forecast; each takes 1-3% off FY2027 EPS.

D-03In relation to the differences between the forecasts over the next 18 months to four years, vs. the last four to six years' financials, how diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences?B588th of 35full answer under 2a Internal risks
D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

B+625th of 35

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. The cone is narrow at EBIT, medium at EPS and wide at the share price. For FY2027 EPS we put the realistic range at about DKK 66.5 to 72.5 around our DKK 70.0 (-5% to +3.5%), with probabilities of about 25% below DKK 68, 55% between DKK 68 and 71.5, and 20% above; the expected value sits about half a point below the base, so the skew is slightly negative. For FY2028 EPS the range is about DKK 70.5 to 81 around DKK 76.4 (-8% to +6%), roughly symmetric, because the two items that can go wrong in 2027 (price against cost, China) have mostly washed through by then while the two that can go right (buyback scale, India) have had time to count. The case is therefore predictable in its earnings over 18 months, to within about ±5% on FY2027 and ±8% on FY2028, which is narrower than the sell-side's own FY2027 dispersion (DKK 65.57 to 75.79, ±7% around the median) and narrower than the cones of Heineken (volume-led), Diageo (price reset) or Royal Unibrew (licence loss and thin hedges). What is not predictable is the multiple, which D-07 shows is mostly set by the sector; the 18-month cone for the share price runs from about DKK 750 (a trough multiple on a downside FY2029 EPS) to about DKK 1,300 (the guideline multiple on an upside EPS), around a mode of about DKK 1,050-1,130. The predictability of the financial forecasts is medium-high; the predictability of the investment outcome is medium-low, and the difference is the multiple.

How we built the EPS cone (FY2027):

Downside (about 25%) Base (about 55%) Upside (about 20%)
What happens Price and mix below 2% against input inflation above 5%; China volume flat to down again in Q2-Q3 2027 with Chongqing's margin still falling; Kazakhstan steps only half way; Sapporo closes late; no buyback until 2028 Price and mix about 2.1% after mix; China volume +2%, EBIT +3-5%; Kazakhstan +DKK 200m; DKK 2bn buyback from August 2027 Price holds at about 3% as the industry passes 2027 costs through; China rebounds on comparatives with cleaner inventories; India lists by H1 2027 and about DKK 4-5bn funds an earlier, larger buyback; finance costs nearer Morgan Stanley's DKK 1.5bn
FY2027 organic EBIT growth About 2.5% 5.0% (lean +0.5) About 7%
FY2027 EBIT (DKK m) About 14,930 15,298 About 15,590
FY2027 EPS (DKK) About 66.5 70.0 About 72.5
FY2028 EPS (DKK) About 70.5 76.4 About 81

The downside EPS loses about DKK 2.4 at EBIT (2.5 points of organic growth), about DKK 0.5 from minorities and finance costs coming in nearer the sell-side's higher figures, and about DKK 0.3 from no buyback; the upside gains about DKK 1.7 at EBIT, DKK 0.5 from an earlier buyback and DKK 0.4 from lower finance costs. The cone is wider below than above at EBIT because the record says management flexes costs to land guidance (which caps the downside at the EBIT line in normal years) but 2027 stacks three headwinds at once (a 160bp input-cost headwind, the Kazakhstan volume lap, the end of most synergies), and because the only year in the record when pricing was tested this hard with a weak consumer, 2022, cost 190bp of gross margin.

Why the cone is narrower than it looks from the drivers. Three features of the business compress it. First, the guidance culture: initial ranges are set 4 points wide and the outcome has landed at or above the top in nearly every year since 2016, so the realised dispersion of organic EBIT against the initial midpoint has been one-sided and the dispersion against the half-year narrowed range has been within about 1 point. Second, the minorities: 40% of Chongqing Brewery, 39% of Lao Brewery and 49% of Carlsberg Malaysia belong to others, so roughly half of any Asian swing never reaches EPS; a 10% swing in Chinese peak-season volume moves group EBIT by about 1-1.5% and EPS by about 0.7%. Third, the fixed financing: 84% of net financial debt is at fixed rates, only DKK 472m of bonds mature within one to two years, and a 1-point rise in rates across currencies costs only DKK 98m a year (annual report 2025); the hybrid coupon is fixed to 2031 and 2034. The three widest lines in the sell-side's models (hybrid, minorities, buyback) are modelling choices, not business uncertainties, and the FY2026 results on 9 February 2027 will narrow them by presenting a full year under IFRS 18 with the coupon shown below net income.

Why the skew is slightly negative for FY2027 and neutral for FY2028. The negatives in 2027 are front-loaded and partly outside control: the first wave of 2027 input costs lands in H1, Chinese comparatives are not easy until Q2, the Kazakhstan volume lap runs all year, and the UK deposit return scheme starts in October 2027 (Rothschild & Co Redburn expects a 12-month slowdown in UK soft drinks). The positives are back-loaded and management-controlled: the buyback (August at the earliest, by management's own words), the full Kazakhstan margin (H2), the easy Chinese comparatives (Q2-Q3). The 18-month window therefore ends on an improving trajectory: H1 2027 is the soft half (our revenue +2.5%, EBIT +4.5%), H2 2027 the firmer one (+3.8%, +5.5%). For a memo written now, the phasing matters as much as the width: the risk of a disappointing print is highest at the Q1 2027 trading statement (late April 2027) and lowest at the H1 2027 results (August 2027).

Peers, for calibration. Heineken's FY2027 depends on volume holding its Q3 2026 pace (+3.9%) under a new chief executive pricing below inflation; its EPS cone is wider on both sides. Diageo's outcomes after its price reset have taken several quarters longer than management expected to stabilise. Royal Unibrew faces a 240bp commodity headwind in 2027 (Morgan Stanley) against Carlsberg's 160bp and Heineken's 150bp, and a 13%-of-revenue licence loss in 2029. AB InBev's cone is the narrowest (consensus met or beaten in 15 of 17 quarters) and it is the one peer the market pays for (P/E at the 46th percentile of its ten years against Carlsberg's 8th). Carlsberg's earnings cone sits closer to AB InBev's than to Heineken's; its multiple does not.

The share-price cone, because the model's return calculation makes it unavoidable. At end-2027 the market will price Carlsberg on FY2029 EPS (24 months forward). Our base FY2029 EPS is about DKK 82.5; the downside path gives about DKK 75-78 and the upside about DKK 86. The multiple's own cone over the last five years runs from 9.95x to 18.83x, with the 10th percentile at 11.3x and the median at 13.8x; the guideline fair multiple is 15.27x. Combining: a downside of about DKK 750-880 (10-11.3x on DKK 75-78), a base of about DKK 1,060-1,130 (12.9-13.8x on DKK 82.5), an upside of about DKK 1,300 (15.3x on DKK 86). From DKK 934, that is -20% to +40% before dividends of about DKK 66 over the period. The mode (+15-20% plus 7% of dividends) is attractive; the width is set by the multiple, not by the earnings, and the memo should say so plainly.

What narrows the cone, and when. By 9 February 2027: FY2026 delivery, the initial FY2027 range, net finance cost guidance, the hybrid's presentation, leverage at end-2026, the Sapporo closing. By late April 2027: Q1 revenue per hectolitre (the pricing test). By August 2027: the Kazakhstan margin in CEEI, China's Q2, the buyback decision. The cone for FY2027 EPS should be about half its present width by August 2027.

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

D4719th of 35

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. Moderately helpful, and less helpful on the drivers that matter most. Over the six months to the end of April 2027, Carlsberg itself will publish profit only once (9 February 2027, the FY2026 results) and volume and revenue twice (that date and the Q1 2027 trading statement in late April). Its disclosure is excellent on the building blocks of guidance (organic EBIT on a stated base, translation, net finance costs, tax, capital expenditure, leverage with a date, synergies, the marketing ratio, the volume and price-mix split) and poor on the four drivers that decide the cone: price and mix are given only at group level, China's profit is not disclosed, the Kazakhstan and soft-drink margins are not disclosed ("we do not guide on regional soft drinks EBIT margins", capital markets day 2025), and minorities are not guided. The gaps are partly filled from outside: two listed subsidiaries (Chongqing Brewery, Carlsberg Malaysia) report quarterly, the Indian draft prospectus gives a level of detail the group never has, and the peers that report before Carlsberg (Heineken, China Resources Beer, Budweiser APAC, Tsingtao) move the read on China. The Consumer can monitor the profit floor well, the pricing test adequately (through revenue per hectolitre by region at Q1), and China, Kazakhstan and the EPS composition only indirectly until February. The result is a case that is easy to monitor on what management controls and hard to monitor on what it does not.

The six-month calendar, and what each event discloses:

When Event What it tells the Consumer What it does not
November-December 2026 Sapporo closing (management: Q4 2026); possible Indian IPO launch (SEBI approved the pre-filing on 1 October; updated draft prospectus 27 October; market chatter of a December launch) Whether end-2026 leverage reaches about 2.5x; the IPO's size (up to 86.42m shares, up to about USD 700m) and valuation; the Indian business's margins (PAT margin 13.7% in FY2026 per the prospectus) The group's use of proceeds, which management has not committed
Monthly Nielsen Europe beer data (Jefferies), Chinese retail sales and on-trade dining metrics, LME aluminium (about USD 3,228 a tonne in Q3 2026; Citi's 2027 average USD 3,500), malting barley (about EUR 240 a tonne) Direction of European mainstream volume, Chinese demand, and the unhedged part of 2027 costs Carlsberg's own price realisation
Late January-February 2027 Chongqing Brewery's preliminary FY2026 results; Carlsberg Malaysia's Q4; Heineken's FY2026 (about 11 February); Budweiser APAC's FY2026 China's Q4: whether inventories stayed normal after the end-Q3 clean-up, whether local brands stabilised, Chongqing's margin; Heineken's China read-across Carlsberg's own China profit (never disclosed)
9 February 2027 Carlsberg FY2026 results Organic EBIT delivery (we expect 5.5-6%); regional EBIT and margins for H2; initial FY2027 guidance (we expect 2-6% or 3-7%); 2027 net finance cost, tax and capex guidance; hedging ratios for 2027 (Jefferies expects them on the call); synergies delivered; leverage; the first full-year P&L under IFRS 18 with the hybrid coupon below net income; the dividend; any buyback signal (management has said an announcement is unlikely) Price and mix by region; China's profit; the Kazakhstan margin; minorities guidance
February-March 2027 European spring price negotiations (France and Germany are the markets Bernstein flags as most resistant); Chinese Spring Festival sell-out Whether 2027 pricing holds in concentrated grocery; first evidence on the Chinese consumer in 2027 Disclosed only through broker channel checks
March 2027 China Resources Beer's FY2026 results; Carlsberg's AGM (dividend paid; last year 16 March) Heineken-brand growth in China (mid-twenties in Q3 2026) against Carlsberg's decline: the share question
Late April 2027 Carlsberg Q1 2027 trading statement; Chongqing Brewery's Q1; Heineken's Q1 Organic volume and revenue by region and category; revenue per hectolitre by region (the 2027 pricing test: above +2% is the base, above +3% the upside, below +2% the downside); China's Q1 against a not-yet-easy comparative; guidance repeated or not Profit; costs; margins

Why the disclosure is weaker in 2026-27 than it was. Three changes have reduced comparability at the moment it matters most. The early adoption of IFRS 18 restated the 2025 base (CPM EBIT DKK 13,686m against the old DKK 13,996m), moved associates below EBIT and bank fees above it, and moved about DKK 0.25-0.4bn between finance costs and operating profit; the hybrid's coupon sits below net income rather than in finance costs; and licence takeovers count as organic, so organic volume in 2026 carries 1-1.5 points of Kazakhstan that will lap in 2027. Bernstein (19 August 2026) called the result "difficult to compare P&L items below operating profit", and the FY2027 EPS spread among brokers shows the cost. The February 2027 results are the first clean full-year presentation; until then, the Consumer should hold the below-EBIT lines as ranges.

The signals worth tracking, with thresholds: (1) Q1 2027 revenue per hectolitre by region: Western Europe above +2%, CEEI above +3%; (2) Chongqing Brewery's Q4 2026 and Q1 2027 volume and operating margin: volume no worse than -5% in Q4 and margin flat year on year in Q1 would say the destocking is over; (3) initial FY2027 guidance midpoint: 4% or above is the base, below 4% the downside; (4) 2027 net finance cost guidance: DKK 1.6bn or below (excluding currency) confirms our finance-cost line; above DKK 1.8bn says the sell-side's higher figures are right; (5) leverage at end-2026: 2.5x or below with Sapporo closed; (6) marketing as a share of revenue in FY2026: above 8.5% would be the first sign that the growth culture is spending the savings; (7) special items and restructuring cash in FY2026 against our DKK 800m.

D-06

How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

C5010th of 35

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. The trace from what management says to EBIT is clear; the trace from EBIT to EPS is the least clear part of the case; and the external transmission is clear in direction but leaks at two points (minorities and the hybrid) that halve or absorb the effect of the dynamics the market watches most. Management's commitments are stated as building blocks that map onto lines: organic EBIT growth on a stated base, translation in kroner, net finance costs, the tax rate, capital expenditure, leverage with a date, the share of synergies each year, a gross-margin target, the marketing ratio, and the one-third volume, two-thirds price-and-mix split of revenue growth. Each block has an identifiable line and a dated milestone. Below EBIT the chain runs through four items the company does not guide (associates, minorities, the hybrid's presentation, the share count), and the sell-side's FY2027 EPS spread of 15% on revenue within 4% is the measure of how much clarity is lost there. The Consumer can trace the thesis onto the P&L to the EBIT line with confidence and must build the rest.

The internal transmission map:

Organic revenue 4-6% Volume one-third, price and mix two-thirds Revenue Never met; not in our base Low as a forecast, high as a stated ambition

The EBIT-to-EPS chain, line by line, for FY2027 (our figures): EBIT 15,298; plus associates 620 (Super Bock, Chinese associates; moved below EBIT by IFRS 18, so a FY2025 comparison must restate); less net finance costs 1,650 all-in (guided only for the current year, excluding currency); less the hybrid coupon 620 gross (presented below net income as "interest on hybrid bonds"; deducted for EPS under IAS 33; cumulative, fixed, non-call 5.25 and 8 years with resets at mid-swaps +1.54% and +1.93% and 100bp step-ups in 2036 and 2039); tax at 23% (guided "about 23%"; H1 2026 22.7%); less minorities 1,280 (not guided; 14.65% of consolidated profit in FY2025; Chongqing 60%, Lao Brewery 61%, Carlsberg Malaysia 51%, plus Sapporo's 25% from 2027); divided by 131.9m average shares (no dilution from incentive shares; buyback timing not guided). Four of the seven steps are unguided. That is why consensus EPS is "about right in level but fragile in composition" (second set), and why the February 2027 results will reshuffle brokers' bridges even if EBIT lands exactly where expected.

The external transmission map:

  • Input costs to EPS. Aluminium (cans are 36-38% of volume), glass (about 10% of cost of goods sold; energy-linked, lagged), barley (stable), energy (hedged 15 months rolling in Western Europe; not hedgeable in Asia and CEEI), diesel. Hedging: at least 70% of barley and aluminium by Q3 of the prior year, 90% at the start of the year, so 2027's cost of goods sold is mostly known by now and the headwind is about 160bp of margin (Morgan Stanley). Transmission to EPS: a 10% aluminium move needs about 1.3% of price; each 0.5 point of price and mix is worth about DKK 300-450m of EBIT (2-3%) and about DKK 2 of EPS. Clear, lagged, and mostly offset by policy.
  • The Chinese consumer to EPS. Weather and on-trade traffic to Chongqing Brewery's volume (on-trade about 50% of its business) to its fixed-cost absorption (Q3 2026: volume -9.6%, gross margin -2.3 points, operating margin -2.5 points, net profit -14%) to Carlsberg's consolidated Asian EBIT, of which 40% belongs to Chongqing's other shareholders. A 10% swing in peak-season Chinese volume is about 1-1.5% of group EBIT and about 0.7% of EPS. Clear, and smaller at EPS than the share-price reaction suggests.
  • Competitors to EPS. Heineken through China Resources Beer (licensed volume +mid-twenties) takes premium share; Heineken and Molson Coors in the UK and Asahi in Poland take mainstream volume on price. Carlsberg's response, conceding volume for margin, converts share loss into a volume line (-1 to -3% mainstream beer a year) rather than a margin line. Clear in direction; its size is visible only in the regional volume disclosure.
  • Currencies and rates to EPS. 92% of revenue in non-krone currencies; a 5% move is about DKK 200m of EBIT; FY2026 translation now +DKK 100m. 84% of debt fixed; refinancing of 18% of gross debt by 2028 against 29% at Heineken; Deutsche Bank's 6.5% refinancing stress costs 1.1% of third-year EPS. Clear and small.
  • Regulation to EPS. Vietnamese excise +5 points a year from 2027 (needs about 3% of price a year; market growth slows to 2-3%); the UK deposit return scheme from October 2027 and the sugar threshold from January 2028; Polish sugar tax from July 2026; Indian state price ceilings (prices need state approval; Telangana's delayed payments carry a provisional USD 30m write-off at a peer). Clear in each market, diffuse in aggregate, tens of basis points of margin.
  • Licences to EPS. PepsiCo awards (Kazakhstan, Kyrgyzstan, Azerbaijan, Georgia, Armenia, the Nordics and Baltics from 2029) add volume at about a 20% discount to the group's price per hectolitre and about 35% gross margin; Coca-Cola's exit from Denmark and Finland at end-2028 removes 4.9m hectolitres and about DKK 1.2bn of gross profit against 3.4m hectolitres and DKK 0.7bn inherited from Royal Unibrew (Rothschild & Co Redburn: -3.3% of group operating profit before mitigation; Jefferies: about DKK 300m of transition costs). Clear, dated, and outside the 18-month window except for its planning costs.

The one transmission the market gets wrong. Deleveraging to EPS. The market reads the cut in net finance cost guidance (DKK 2.2bn to 1.8bn) as a saving; about DKK 150-250m of it is the IFRS 18 reclassification of bank fees and trade-loan items to operating profit (EPS-neutral), and the hybrid's coupon (DKK 477m after tax) now sits below net income where several models do not deduct it (Jefferies: the drop in finance costs is "entirely offset"). The combined cost of debt plus hybrid falls only from about DKK 2.34bn to 2.27bn between 2026 and 2027. From 2028 the balance sheet adds to EPS through the share count, not through interest. The memo should trace this explicitly, because it is the point at which the sell-side's bridges disperse and at which the February 2027 results will force a correction.

C-82

What perception- or sentiment-driven risks are there that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere in the sector, theme or market that could hit the share price whatever the company's own fundamentals?

B357th of 35

Layer 1 · Risks

The Investment Analyst's heading What perception- or sentiment-driven risks are there that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere in the sector, theme or market?

Judgement. Sentiment risk is elevated because the October rise was a rotation trade without fundamental support. Brewers rose 9.6-17.4% from 1 to 22 October. Carlsberg's average target price moved -0.2% while its price rose 14.1%, and target-price breadth was -21%. The rise has already started to unwind: -3% on the Q3 trading statement.

Specific triggers:

  • A reversal of the defensive rotation. The Fitness for Fighting digest puts Momentum at Underweight. If the US 10-year yield falls below 4.5% or risk appetite returns, defensives give back.
  • Read-across from peers' China figures. Heineken, China Resources Beer and Budweiser APAC report before Carlsberg.
  • Alcohol-health headlines: WHO policy, cancer labelling, weight-loss drugs.
  • US tariff rhetoric. It de-rated the sector in late April 2026, taking 2-4 points off on top of a company-specific fall.
  • European sovereign stress (France, Italy) adding a risk premium.
  • Further broker downgrades:
  • Jefferies moved to Hold in September;
  • the buy share fell 4 points to 59%;
  • DNB Carnegie has a trading sell;
  • Barclays was at Sell (DKK 775) on 10 September. FactSet's broker table now shows it at Hold (DKK 930), but that table has known column shifts, so the change needs checking.
  • Foundation governance headlines.

Typical size. 5-15% moves without a change in fundamentals. Liquidity is not the problem, at about USD 55-65m traded a day.

Track record (guidance raise/sell-side upgrades) ~L2-3Y

C-61How strong is the company's record of acting, not just talking: a recent record of huge delivery and action, and a mid-term track record of delivery, not misses or justifications, whatever the environment (outperforming in tougher ones is even better), beyond its records against guidance and estimates?B+805th of 35full answer under 4b Management quality
C-70

Beyond meeting or beating, what is the record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance?

A822nd of 35

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement. The pattern is consistent:

  • a wide, conservative initial range (about 4 points wide);
  • narrowed upward at the half year (in 2023, 2024, 2025 and 2026);
  • delivered at or above the top.
Year Initial guidance Revised at H1 Delivered
2024 1-5% Raised to 4-6% 6.0%
2025 1-5% Narrowed to 3-5% 5.0%
2026 2-6% Narrowed to 4-6% (19 August) Reiterated 29 October

The beat against the initial midpoint is shrinking:

  • about 300bp in 2024;
  • 200bp in 2025;
  • probably 150-200bp in 2026.

That compares with Jefferies' 300-870bp a year since 2016.

Nature. The upgrades come from narrowing ranges, not from raising the top. Revenue is never formally guided year by year. The current chief executive has kept the culture: Deutsche Bank's notes are titled "Underpromise, overdeliver" and "Carlsberg don't do disappointments".

Implication. The habit protects against disappointment, but it no longer creates consensus upgrades, because the sell-side has learned to sit at or near the top. Do not model a beat larger than about 1 point on organic EBIT.

  • the 2025 volume fall: profit grew 5%;
  • the 2026 Chinese floods: H1 profit beat.

P-06

After assessing the track record of delivery in greater detail, how much weight should the forecasts for the next 18 months to four years carry, and in which direction should they be leaned?

C+589th of 35

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement. The track record splits sharply by line, and the weights and leans should split the same way:

  • EBIT. One of the most reliable records among European brewers: organic EBIT at or above the top of the initial range in nearly every year since 2016, through cost spikes, volume falls and floods.
  • Revenue. Consistently below the company's own ambition: the 4-6% organic target has never been met.
  • Volume. Below consensus in most recent quarters.
  • Projects, synergies and deleveraging. Ahead of plan.
  • EPS. Far below what the operating record would suggest over 2019-25, because of Russia, Britvic financing and interest. On the Viewforth definition EPS has been flat at DKK 64-65 since 2022.
Line Weight FY2026 Weight FY2027 Weight FY2028-30 Lean against our base
Organic EBIT and EBIT margin High (90%; H2 only remains, guidance repeated 29-Oct-26) Medium-high (70%) Medium (50%) Up slightly: +0.3 to +0.5% a year
Revenue and volume High (85%) Medium (55%) Low-medium (40%) Down: revenue -0.2 to -0.3 points a year; volume lower
Below-EBIT lines (finance, minorities, hybrid, associates) Medium-high (75%) Medium (50%) Low-medium (40%) Two-sided; minorities is the risk
Buyback timing and size Not applicable Low (35%) Low (35%) Later rather than earlier in 2027; size up if India lists
Free cash flow High (80%) Medium (60%) Medium (50%) Slightly up on working capital, with supplier finance as the tail risk
EPS High (85%) Medium (60%) Low-medium (40%) Neutral for FY2027; slightly up for FY2028 on capital returns

The profit record:

Year Initial guidance Revised at H1 Delivered Beat against initial midpoint
2023 About 5.2% organic (reported hit by -8.4% currency)
2024 1-5% Raised to 4-6% 6.0% About 300bp
2025 1-5% Narrowed to 3-5% 5.0% About 200bp
2026 2-6% Narrowed to 4-6% We expect about 5.5-6% About 150-200bp

Jefferies counts beats of 300-870bp a year since 2016. The beat is shrinking, which matters for upgrades, not for delivery. The mechanism is controllable: when volume or costs disappoint, opex is flexed (2020: -14% organically) and synergies or pricing are pulled forward.

  • For FY2026: our 5.75% organic sits between the midpoint (5%) and the top (6%), +0.7% above consensus EBIT.
  • For FY2027: the record suggests organic EBIT of at least 5% is close to a floor under normal conditions. Our base is 5.0% because 2027 stacks a 160bp cost headwind, the Kazakhstan volume lap and the end of most synergies. Lean slightly up, by about +0.5%, on management's habit of delivering, but not to the 7-9% that "high-single-digit compounding" would imply. The company has not delivered high-single-digit organic EBIT growth since the Funding the Journey years.

The revenue record:

  • The 2023-25 incentive plan's organic revenue element paid 12.10 of 20.00 against a 4% target.
  • The ten-year organic compound rate is 3.1%.
  • H1 2026 was 2.7% and Q3 2026 3.0%.
  • Volume missed consensus in Q1 price and mix (0.8% against 1.4%), H1 (+1.7% against +1.9%), Q2 (+0.8% against +1.2%) and Q3 (-0.4% against +0.6%), while revenue beat in Q1 and Q3.

So revenue forecasts above about 3.5% organic should be discounted. Our 2.9-3.2% organic is consistent with the record. Consensus at 3.7-3.8% (FY2027-28) needs better-than-record delivery. Lean revenue down against consensus, and treat our own revenue as fair to slightly high in FY2027.

The execution record:

  • Britvic synergies: about 80% by end-2026 against a five-year plan; target raised from GBP 100m to GBP 110m.
  • Kazakhstan plant opened on schedule.
  • Leverage from 3.9x to 3.0x in twelve months.
  • Sapporo agreed at 21.3x EBIT.

These justify high weight on dated, management-controlled steps (Sapporo closing, Kazakhstan margin step, last synergies). The record is weaker on value-chain-dependent steps: premium in China, Polish and UK mainstream share, alcohol-free monetisation.

The EPS record, and what it means for weight. Adjusted EPS grew about 5.5% a year from 2023 to 2025. The Viewforth-definition EPS was 54.74 (2019) and 65.11 (2025): 2.9% a year. The forecasts call for about 8% a year. The acceleration is credible because its sources are mechanical rather than heroic: the Britvic financing is already in the base; Russia is gone; the synergies are banked; buybacks restart. But it is unproven at EPS level. The company has not compounded EPS at 8% since before Russia. Weight FY2028-30 EPS at about 40% and the near years higher.

Where the record cuts against the first set and the memo. The memo's modal EPS path (DKK 70.7 in FY2027, 9.2% a year) requires revenue at 3.7%, EBITDA growth of 5.6% a year and a buyback from 2027. The record supports the buyback and the EBIT discipline. It does not support 3.7% revenue or EBITDA compounding at 5.6% once synergies end. Lean the memo's path down by about 1.5 points a year. Our base is at consensus, and the record's lean (EBIT up slightly, revenue down) roughly cancels at EPS level.

Forecasts with good growth rates and higher growth/margins than past

Memo topic 6, Forecast financials: the Rater's score

7a

Growth rate

Group judgement

Financial forecasts

F4427th of 35

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The financial forecasts describe a company whose profits are more predictable than its revenue, and whose EPS is more predictable in total than in composition.

What drives the forecasts:

  • Revenue. About 3% a year, driven by price, with volume near zero. Kazakhstan laps in 2027, soft-drink mix dilutes revenue per hectolitre and the China recovery reverses 2026's favourable geographic mix. Revenue is the line most likely to come in below consensus.
  • EBIT. About 5% a year, delivered by a management that has landed at or above the top of its profit guidance nearly every year since 2016 and flexes costs to do so. More than half of EBIT growth in FY2026-27 comes from named self-help (Britvic synergies, Kazakhstan in-housing, restructuring), and that self-help is largely spent by end-2027. From FY2028 the case depends on price staying ahead of costs, which the record shows is nominal, not real.
  • Below EBIT. Mixed:
  • the hybrid coupon absorbs most of the interest saving;
  • minorities rise with the Sapporo sale and the Asian recovery;
  • the tax rate stabilises at about 23%;
  • the buyback, restarting in H2 2027 and running at DKK 4-5bn a year from 2028, becomes the largest below-EBIT driver of EPS.

The result:

  • EPS of DKK 65.70, 69.52, 75.97, 82.12 and 89.92 for FY2026-30: about 8% a year, at consensus in level but not in route;
  • FY2027 growth of about 6% as the pinch year;
  • free cash flow rising from about DKK 7.2bn to 11.2bn;
  • leverage held at about 2.2-2.5x.

For the third set:

  • Drivers. They are concentrated: price against cost in 2027, China's 2027 recovery, and the buyback date together explain most of the FY2027-28 cone.
  • Disclosure. It is adequate on profit (half-yearly, with clear building blocks) and poor on price by region, China's profit, minorities and the hybrid's EPS treatment.
  • Predictability. High for FY2026, which is H2 only and repeated guidance. Medium for FY2027. Low-medium for FY2028-30.

For the fourth set:

  • Guidance. Expect FY2027 guidance on 9 February 2027 to open wide with a midpoint at or below consensus. That is a sentiment risk more than an estimate risk.
  • Consensus. Exposed to modest revenue cuts and to below-EBIT reshuffling (hybrid, minorities). Supported by the unmodelled buyback.

The likely net effect on the investment case is neutral to mildly positive. The forecasts support a steady compounder, not a re-rating.

P-08

After assessing below-operating-profit cost items (e.g. tax, interest, one-offs, minority investments, etc.) and share buybacks or issuance in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share, net income and free cash flow over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

C5014th of 35

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement. Over FY2019-25 the lines below EBIT and the share count worked in opposite directions:

  • Against EPS: net interest rose from DKK 0.75bn to DKK 2.64bn (Britvic, higher rates); special items swelled (DKK 1.9bn in 2025); the tax rate was erratic (19.6-27.6%).
  • For EPS: buybacks of about DKK 20bn cut the share count from 148.0m to 132.2m (-1.9% a year).

Over FY2026-30 the picture changes in three ways:

  1. Interest becomes a modest tailwind in 2026-27 and then flat, but the hybrid coupon absorbs much of it.
  2. Minority interests become a growing headwind. The Sapporo joint venture adds about DKK 150m from 2027, and Asian recovery flows partly to minorities, so minorities rise from about DKK 1.18bn to 1.48bn.
  3. The share count resumes falling from H2 2027, at about 2.5-3.5% a year in 2028-30, faster than in 2019-24.

Net effect: EPS grows about 1.5 points faster than EBIT in FY2026 and only about 0.8 points faster in FY2027, the pinch year. From FY2028 it grows about 4 points a year faster: financial gearing, net of rising minorities, adds about 1-1.5 points, and the share count adds about 2.5-3.5 points. The share count, not interest, is the main below-EBIT driver of EPS from FY2028.

Where we differ from consensus below EBIT (FY2027 EPS: ours DKK 69.52, consensus DKK 69.25):

  • Interest. Consensus implies net finance costs of about DKK 1.9bn in FY2027 (falling only about DKK 150m a year). We expect about DKK 1.65bn all-in. The difference is about +DKK 1.5 of EPS in our favour.
  • Minorities. We carry them higher, including the Sapporo charge that Bernstein puts at only DKK 40m and we put at about DKK 150m: about -DKK 1 of EPS against brokers who use Bernstein's figure.
  • Buyback. About +DKK 0.3 in FY2027 and about +DKK 2 in FY2028. Consensus carries a flat share count of about 132m.
  • EBIT. Ours is DKK 26m below consensus in FY2027 and about DKK 150m below in FY2028.

So at EPS level we arrive at consensus by a different route: lower revenue, lower FY2028 EBIT, cheaper financing, larger minorities and a buyback. For the fourth set, this means consensus EPS is about right in level but fragile in composition. If the buyback slips to 2028, FY2028 EPS is about 2.5% below consensus. If the India listing proceeds and is bought back, it is about 2% above.

  3. The tail risks to free cash flow are working capital, not profit. Supplier finance of DKK 3.2bn (terms up to 180 days) could reverse, a one-off outflow of up to DKK 3bn. Days sales outstanding have risen from 24 to 31. We assume working capital erodes by about 0.7 points of revenue over five years (from -13.5% to -12.8%).

One-offs. Under CPM, restructuring and integration costs no longer appear as "special items" in adjusted EBIT, but they still cost cash: about DKK 0.8bn in 2026, falling to about DKK 0.25bn by 2030. The Nordic transition (2028-29) and any new efficiency programme would add to them. Reported (IFRS) net income will stay about DKK 0.8-1.2bn below adjusted net income: PPA amortisation of about DKK 640m a year on the Britvic intangibles (brands DKK 4.4bn, contract-related assets DKK 9.3bn), plus restructuring.

Judgement. Our base case is a self-help and capital-return compounder with no demand growth:

C-78

Beyond cutting costs, how much plausible scope is there to improve the financial outputs over the mid term; do peer precedents show it is achievable; would it come from a few big areas or many small ones; and is it congruent with the stated strategy?

F5826th of 35

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement. There is plausible scope for about +100-150bp of operating margin over three to four years, from 15.6% to about 16.5-17%. Below operating profit, finance costs and buybacks add to that.

Sources:

  • A few big areas:
  • Britvic synergies;
  • Kazakhstan in-housing;
  • Teisseire;
  • the gross-margin rebuild (pricing, mix, procurement).
  • Many small ones:
  • VMx revenue management;
  • digital tools;
  • logistics;
  • the alcohol-free mix gain (no duty).

This is congruent with the strategy, which says the margin rebuild funds marketing. DNB Carnegie's long-term 17.1-17.3% is about 2 points above history, at the upper end.

Peer precedents:

  • Carlsberg added 300bp in 2015-19.
  • Heineken added 41bp in FY2025.
  • AB InBev added 101bp of organic EBITDA margin in FY2025.

Constraints:

  • soft-drink mix, about -30bp a year;
  • the marketing step-up;
  • 2027 costs.

For the model. About +30bp a year of operating margin in FY2027-28 (consensus), with upside from Kazakhstan and pricing and downside from China and costs.

C-80

To what extent has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months: is the revenue growth rate running below the company's own normal rate, and is the input change force itself slowing?

F3827th of 35

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement. Partly yes.

The core engine has slowed. Premium beer was meant to deliver two-thirds of revenue growth through price and mix:

  • Premium volume grew 5% in 2025 (excluding San Miguel), 3% in Q1 2026 and 1% in H1 2026.
  • 1664 Blanc grew 2% in Q1, then fell 1% in H1 and 5% in Q3.
  • Chinese volume fell 6% in Q2 and about 10% in Q3.

Revenue growth is below the company's normal rate. It was 2.7% in H1 2026 and 3.0% in Q3, against a 4-6% target, a 3-5% earlier algorithm and a ten-year organic compound rate of 3.1%. It is stable rather than falling.

The input change force is maturing:

  • Britvic synergies are about 80% done by end-2026.
  • The Kazakhstan volume boost laps in 2027.

The replacement engine is not slowing, but its pace is flattered. Soft drinks grew 9% in H1, or 4% excluding Kazakhstan, against Britvic's five-year volume growth of 1.5%.

For the model. 2027 revenue growth must come from price (linked to local inflation) and easy Chinese comparatives while Kazakhstan laps. Consensus at +3.8% is at the upper edge of what that supports.

P-04After assessing the pricing power in greater detail, how is price likely to contribute to revenue and margin over the next 18 months to four years?C4613th of 35full answer under 5a Pricing power
P-09

After assessing the other questions/items in the set in greater detail, what is the net likely impact to the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

D4526th of 35

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement. The next five years look like a different company from the last six, but the difference sits almost entirely in the profit and per-share lines, not in revenue:

  • Revenue. Grows more slowly than the reported history (3.2% a year against 5.2% over FY2019-25), because that history was inflated by Britvic and depressed by the Russia exit. It grows in line with the underlying organic record (about 3%).
  • EBIT and margin. EBIT grows at about 5% a year with the margin rising about 30bp a year. Over FY2019-25 the margin was roughly flat on the company's basis and down on FactSet's.
  • EPS. Compounds at about 8% a year against about 3% a year on the Viewforth definition over FY2019-25. The gap is not demand. It is the end of three drags (Russia, the cost of financing Britvic, special items) and the return of buybacks.
  • Cash. Free cash flow roughly doubles from the FY2019-25 average, as Britvic's cash flow, lower capex intensity after 2026 and fading restructuring cash combine.

The judgement for the Consumer: the forecasts rely on things management controls (synergies, plants, financing, buybacks) and on price keeping up with costs. They do not rely on volume. That makes the next 18 months predictable in direction but sensitive in magnitude to two inputs: 2027 price against cost, and the buyback date.

History against forecast (compound annual rates; bases named):

Line FY2019-25 (history) FY2025-30 (forecast) What changes
Revenue, reported +5.2% (65.9bn to 89.1bn; Britvic in, Russia out) +3.2% (to 104.3bn) No large M&A licence takeovers add about 0.3 points a year
Revenue, organic About 3% (ten-year rate 3.1%) About 3.0% Unchanged: no demand inflection; price two-thirds or more

Concentration of the drivers. We decomposed the change in EPS from FY2025 (DKK 60.81) to FY2028 (DKK 75.97), +DKK 15.2 (+25%):

Driver Contribution
Britvic synergies (2026-27) About +DKK 4.0

China (down then up) About 0
Interest saved About +DKK 4.5
Hybrid coupon About -DKK 3.6
Minorities (Sapporo and Asia) About -DKK 1.8
Associates About 0
Buyback About +DKK 2.3

The rows are approximate and do not sum exactly, because per-share effects interact with the buyback. Three drivers (synergies, underlying price-led growth, net financing) supply about 80% of the change. Two of them (synergies, financing) are complete or nearly complete by end-2027, which is why the third set should test the price-against-cost driver hardest.

What this means for the 18-month window (H2 2026 to H2 2027):

  • H2 2026: revenue +4.0%, EBITDA margin about 21.6% (H2 2025 about 21.1%), on synergies and currency.
  • H1 2027: revenue +2.5%, margin about 21.5%. The Kazakhstan volume lap, the first wave of 2027 costs and Chinese comparatives that are not yet easy in Q1 make this the soft half.
  • H2 2027: revenue +3.8%, margin about 21.8%, on easy Chinese comparatives and the full Kazakhstan margin. The UK deposit scheme from October 2027 is a small drag.

The two dated tests: initial FY2027 guidance on 9 February 2027, which we expect to be a wide range of about 2-6% or 3-7% organic EBIT with a midpoint of 4-5%, at or slightly below the FY2027 consensus of 5.9%; and the H1 2027 results in August 2027, where the buyback is most likely to be announced.

Forecasts and upgrades

8

Probably upgrades this financial year to

C-4217th of 36

Memo topic 7, Scope for upgrades over 6 to 12 months: the Rater's score

Behind the rating as a whole

Group judgement

The fourth set as a whole

C+4611th of 35

Layer 4 · The fourth set as a whole

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

Our assessment is already reflected in guidance, sell-side estimates and the narrative at the level that matters least, the EPS total, and not reflected in the parts that matter most for the next 18 months, the order in which the parts get corrected. Guidance will be met and will open conservatively; consensus EPS is at our level but is composed of too much revenue and too little below-the-line leakage, with no buyback; the narrative knows the story and under-models its mechanics. The sequence this produces is a market that is likely to be disappointed by composition in February-April 2027 (the opening range below consensus, the first clean IFRS 18 year showing the coupon, revenue cut towards 3%, sell-side breadth at its worst) and then reassured by delivery and capital returns from August 2027 (the H1 beat, the upward narrowing, the buyback, a share count that consensus has not modelled). The gap between our assessment and the market is therefore small in level, unfavourable in near-term timing and favourable in the second half of the window; the things the market does not know are the pinch year (negative, first) and the buyback's accretion (positive, second).

The steer for the memo. Topic #4: guidance is conservative by habit and the habit is known, so it supports delivery, not upgrades. Topic #7: scope for upgrades over 6-12 months is nil at the EPS line and negative at the revenue line; the upgrade is the share count, and it is dated August 2027. Topic #8: momentum is D on the sell-side and D technically, and both are more likely to worsen before they improve. Topic #10: the stock is turning up on the balance sheet and stalled on volume, which puts it at "bottoming to turning up", and the turn is dated by the buyback. Topics #11 and #12: use the corrected EPS (DKK 70.0 for FY2027, 76.4 for FY2028, 82.5 for FY2029), present the multiple as about two-thirds sector-driven with a cone of 11-15x, and show the 18-month return as earnings and dividends the company controls (about +15-20% to end-2027 on an unchanged multiple) plus a re-rating the company can only half control. The investment case is a patient one: the entry is more favourable after the February-April 2027 corrections than before them, unless the Indian listing lands in December and brings the buyback forward.

8a

Guidance

3 answers
C-1360C-6852I-0155

C-68To what extent is company guidance above or below sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?C5212th of 35full answer under 6a Guidance conservatism
I-01

How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all such events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

C+5510th of 35

Layer 4 · Guidance

Judgement. Guidance will almost certainly be met in FY2026, opened conservatively for FY2027 on 9 February 2027 with a midpoint below consensus, narrowed upward in August 2027 and met again in February 2028. That is the pattern of 2023, 2024, 2025 and 2026, and nothing in the evidence says it breaks: management manages to its number, and the number it sets is one it can beat. The sequence is favourable for delivery and unfavourable for sentiment at one event, the February 2027 opening, when a 2-6% or 3-7% range for FY2027 will sit against a FactSet consensus of +5.7-5.9% EBIT growth and a market that has just been reminded by the Q3 volume miss that the revenue algorithm is not being met. The probability that FY2027 guidance is reduced during 2027 is about 10-15%, concentrated in a Chinese relapse in Q2-Q3 against the 2026 floods and in 2027 pricing failing to cover costs. The probability that it is raised above its initial top is about 10%; the usual "raise" is the upward narrowing, which we put at 60-65% in August 2027. On the mid-term framework, the leverage target will be met a year early, the "operating profit faster than revenue" promise will be kept, the 4-6% revenue target will be missed again in 2026 and 2027 and has about a 40% chance of being reset at the next strategy cycle (likely a capital markets day by 2028), and the 47-49% gross-margin target will not be reached by 2028. The informal "high-single-digit operating profit compounding" is the ambition most likely to be quietly dropped.

Event by event:

Event Most likely outcome Probability What drives the other outcomes
FY2026 results, 9 February 2027: delivery against 4-6% organic EBIT, +DKK 100m translation, DKK 1.8bn net finance costs, 23% tax, DKK 6-7bn capex Met, at 5.5-6.0%; FactSet consensus (5% plus currency, DKK 14,472m) beaten by about 1%; the Bloomberg consensus of about 5.8% roughly met Met within range about 92%; at or above 6% about 50%; below 4% about 3% H1 was +5.9%; Q3 revenue beat; synergies about 80% done; China's Q4 is the only line that could pull the figure below 5.5%, and management has already flexed costs in such quarters (2020, 2025)
FY2027 initial guidance, same day A wide range of 2-6% or 3-7% organic EBIT, midpoint 4-5%; net finance costs guided at about DKK 1.5-1.7bn excluding currency; tax about 23%; capex DKK 6-7bn; translation small positive; the hybrid coupon stated separately; leverage reported at or near 2.5x; no buyback announced but an intention to resume "in the course of 2027" signalled Midpoint below consensus about 70%; a narrower or higher range (4-7%) about 15%; a lower one (1-5%) about 15%; buyback announced about 15% Bernstein (21 August 2026) reported the chief executive "firmly expected mid-single-digit EBIT growth" for 2027; the company has never opened above a 4-point range under this chief executive; Jefferies (29 October) and management (Q3 call) both say a February buyback is unlikely because of the March dividend, seasonal cash and PepsiCo investments; an Indian listing closed before February is the only thing that would change that
Q1 2027 trading statement, late April 2027 Guidance repeated; revenue per hectolitre by region the only new evidence Repeated about 85%; cut about 10%; raised about 5% A Q1 revenue per hectolitre below +2% with input inflation visible would be the first warning; Chinese comparatives are not yet easy (Q1 2026 was not a flood quarter)
H1 2027 results, August 2027 Range narrowed upward (to 4-6% or 4-7%); buyback of about DKK 2bn announced for H2; CEEI margin shows the Kazakhstan step Narrowed upward about 60-65%; held about 25%; cut about 10-15%; buyback announced about 60% (about 80% if India has listed, about 45% if not) The pattern held in 2023, 2024, 2025 and 2026; the cut case needs China's Q2 to fall against a -6% comparative and price to miss costs at the same time; the buyback needs leverage comfortably below 2.5x after the March dividend, which the Sapporo cash (DKK 4.2bn, 0.2x) secures if it closed in Q4 2026
Q3 2027 trading statement, late October 2027 Guidance repeated at the narrowed range About 85% China's Q3 lap of the 2026 floods is the swing; the UK deposit return scheme starts on 1 October 2027
FY2027 results and FY2028 initial guidance, February 2028 FY2027 delivered at or above the top of the narrowed range (about 5-6%); FY2028 opened at 2-6% or 3-7% against a consensus of about +5.8%, midpoint again below consensus; a larger buyback (DKK 4bn) and leverage about 2.4x; possibly the announcement of a new strategy cycle Delivered at or above the narrowed top about 70%; FY2028 midpoint below consensus about 65% FY2028 is the first year without named self-help; a conservative opening is even more likely than in 2027

What would drive a raise above the initial top in 2027 (about 10%). All three of the following together: China's volume up mid-single digits in Q2-Q3 on comparatives with Chongqing's margin recovering; 2027 price and mix at 3% or above as the whole industry passes aluminium and glass inflation through (Royal Unibrew is already guiding to a further 3% of price in early 2027; the Coca-Cola system has a consistent record of pricing for packaging costs); the Kazakhstan business reaching group-average margins in 2027 rather than 2028. That combination is our upside case (7% organic EBIT) and it would also bring the India-funded buyback forward.

What would drive a cut (about 10-15%). China is the first candidate: Rothschild & Co Redburn has the Chinese operating margin falling a further 100bp in 2027 and expert consensus attributes 2026's fall more to consumer confidence than to weather; a second weak summer, with on-trade at 47.5% of the market and still falling, would take 1-1.5% off group EBIT at the peak and remove the comparatives tailwind on which our +DKK 100m rests. Costs are the second: the 160bp headwind is mostly hedged, but glass contracts for 2027 were still being negotiated in October 2026 and aluminium at about USD 3,500 a tonne in 2027 (Citi) is above the level in the hedges. Retail resistance in France and Germany is the third. A cut would most likely come at the H1 results, as a narrowing to the lower half rather than a reduction of the range, which is how 2025's 1-5% became 3-5%.

The mid-term framework, 18 months to three years out:

  • Organic revenue 4-6% a year. Missed in every year since it was set and will be missed in 2026 (about 3%) and 2027 (about 2.9% in our base; Jefferies 2.3%, Rothschild & Co Redburn 3.0%). Probability it is formally reduced by 2028: about 40%, most likely at a new strategy cycle where a "3-5%" or a volume-and-value split replaces it; probability it is raised: nil. The more likely outcome is that it is left in place and not met, as 2023-25's plan paid out 12.10 of 20.00 on it.
  • Operating profit growing faster than revenue. Met in 2024, 2025 and 2026, and met in our base throughout (about 5% against about 3%). Probability met in 2027-28: about 80%.
  • Gross margin of 47-49% pro forma. Company-basis gross margin was 45.2% in 2025 and 45.7% in H1 2026; our base holds it at about 45.5% to 2030 because 2027 costs are priced through in money, not in margin, and soft-drink mix takes about 30bp a year. Probability reached by 2028: about 25%; probability the target is dropped or pushed out: about 50%.
  • Leverage below 2.5x by end-2027. Reached at about 2.5x at end-2026 if Sapporo closes in Q4 2026 (management: it will), about 2.7x if not; met by end-2027 with about 90% probability either way. Management has said 2.5x is "fit for the future business", so it is unlikely to be tightened.
  • Marketing at 8.5-9% of revenue. 8.3% in 2025; we have it reaching 8.5% by 2028. Likely met slowly; a faster step would be an EPS negative (DKK 650m, 4% of EBIT, to reach 9% at once).
  • The informal "high-single-digit operating profit compounding over the next three years" (October 2025 capital markets day) and "MSD+ organic EBIT growth" (August 2026 roadshow). Our base is 5%; consensus 5.7-5.9%. The ambition is above both and is unlikely to be formalised. Probability it is repeated at the next capital markets day in that form: about 40%.

What this means for the gap between our assessment and guidance. Guidance, as it will stand in February 2027, is likely to be below our FY2027 base (midpoint 4-5% against our 5.0% with a slight upward lean) and well below the informal ambition; the formal framework beyond 2027 is more conservative than our base on leverage and the profit-faster-than-revenue promise, and more aggressive than our base on revenue and gross margin. The favourable part of the gap is the habit: a company that opens below what it expects to deliver gives the memo a floor it can rely on. The unfavourable part is that the sell-side has learned the habit and sits at the top of the range, so the floor no longer produces upgrades; and that the first event in the window is the one most likely to disappoint.

Judgement. We expect the order to be down, then up: FY2027 consensus EPS is more likely to be trimmed by 1-3% between now and the Q1 2027 statement (revenue cuts towards 3%, below-the-line clean-up after the first IFRS 18 full year, an initial guidance midpoint below consensus), then held through the summer, then raised by 1-3% from August 2027 as the H1 beat, the upward narrowing and the buyback announcement arrive. The net change in FY2027 consensus over the 18 months is about nil to -2%; in FY2028 about -1% to +2%, because the buyback (not in consensus, whose share count is flat at about 132m) adds more to the outer year than the revenue cuts take away. The breadth of revisions follows the same path: negative into the spring (buy share 59% and down 4 points in three months, Jefferies' move to Hold in September, revision-momentum score -7), neutral in the summer, positive from August. For 18 months to three years out, FY2029 rests on two brokers (DKK 82.73; our 82.45) and will form as coverage extends; it is more likely to be raised than cut once the buyback is modelled, by 2-4%, provided EBIT growth holds at about 5%, and more likely to be cut if FY2028 shows that EBIT growth without named self-help is nearer 3-4%.

The revision sequence over the 18 months, event by event:

  1. Now to 9 February 2027. FY2026 EPS stable to +1% (delivery at 5.5-6%). FY2027 revenue trimmed by the houses still at 3.7-3.8% (the median falls towards 3.3-3.5%); FY2027 EPS -1% to -2% on the mean as Nordea (DKK 67.10), Barclays (Hold, DKK 930 target) and the unnamed brokers below DKK 68 are joined by others reacting to the Q3 volume miss and Chinese Q4 read-across (Chongqing's preliminary results in January). Breadth negative: the buy share, already down 4 points to 59% in three months, could fall to the low fifties if one or two of the remaining Buys (Goldman Sachs, UBS, Morgan Stanley, Deutsche Bank, DNB Carnegie, Oddo, Jyske) move on valuation after October's +14%.
  2. 9 February 2027. Two forces pull in opposite directions. The opening range's midpoint below consensus and the first clean IFRS 18 full year push the EPS mean down (the hybrid and minorities clean-up is worth about DKK 1-2 a share at the brokers who have not done it); the delivery at 6%, a net finance cost guide at or below DKK 1.6bn (worth +DKK 1-1.5 a share against brokers at DKK 1.8-1.9bn), and the leverage print at about 2.5x pull it up. Net: FY2027 EPS mean -1% to -2%; FY2028 -2% to -3% (revenue and the end of synergies); FY2029 forms around DKK 80-83 as coverage extends from two brokers.
  3. Late April 2027. The pricing test. Revenue per hectolitre at or above +2% holds consensus; below +2% with input costs visible cuts FY2027 EBIT 1-2% at the houses that have waited.
  4. August 2027. The H1 beat against a conservative range, the upward narrowing and the buyback announcement (about 60%). FY2027 EPS +0.5-1%; FY2028 EPS +2-3% as the share count enters the models; the buy share recovers; the revision-momentum score turns positive. This is the first event at which Carlsberg's sell-side momentum could move from D towards B.
  5. October 2027 to February 2028. China's Q3 lap and the FY2027 delivery hold the numbers; the FY2028 opening below consensus repeats the February 2027 sentiment pattern, but with a buyback running and the share count falling, the EPS mean no longer falls with it.

Why the level of consensus is right and its composition is wrong, and why that matters for the memo. Our EPS arrives at consensus by a different route: lower revenue and FY2028 EBIT, cheaper financing, larger minorities and a buyback. Each of those differences is a dated correction the sell-side will make, and the corrections net to about zero at the EPS line. The memo's topic #7 (scope for upgrades over the coming 6-12 months) should therefore say: little at the EPS line, negative at the revenue line, positive at the share-count line from August 2027; and the sequence (cuts first) argues that the sell-side's momentum will be at its worst at the moment of the February 2027 results and improve from there. The precedents cut both ways. The out-year consensus at Carlsberg has a history of being cut as the year approaches (FY2027 from a peak of DKK 79.81 to 69.72, FY2028 from 91.19 to 76.12 over the series), though much of that was the Britvic financing and the IFRS 18 base being modelled; the in-year consensus has a history of being met or beaten. AB InBev's precedent (consensus met or beaten in 15 of 17 quarters, a buyback restart, sell-side momentum A) is the one Carlsberg can follow from August 2027 if China cooperates.

18 months to three years out. FY2029 consensus is two brokers (DKK 82.73 EPS, DKK 17,518m EBIT, which is +8% on FY2028 and too high; our DKK 16,706m). As coverage builds, the EBIT line is more likely to be cut (the Nordic swap costs in 2029, no synergies, revenue at 3%) and the EPS line more likely to be raised (buybacks of DKK 4-5bn a year, about 2.5-3.5 points of EPS growth a year, in no consensus model yet; India's proceeds another 2% if bought back). Net: FY2029 EPS consensus more likely up 2-4% than down, conditional on EBIT growth of about 5% in FY2028. If FY2028 shows EBIT growth of 3-4% without a new programme, the cut comes first and the buyback only softens it.

The breadth of revisions. Nineteen brokers forecast FY2027 EPS; ten of the seventeen rated are at Buy or Overweight (59%), seven at Hold, none at Sell on FactSet's table (Barclays moved from Sell at DKK 775 in September to Hold at DKK 930, subject to the table's column shift). The net breadth of EPS revisions has been zero to -1% over one to six months, EBITDA -2%, target prices -1% over three months. The direction of breadth over the next six months is negative for the reasons above; over months six to eighteen it is positive. For the memo's topic #8 (current momentum), the honest description is: sell-side momentum D now, likely F-to-D in the spring, likely C-to-B after August 2027.

The gap between our assessment and consensus. Neutral at the EPS line, favourable at the share-count and finance-cost lines, unfavourable at the revenue and minorities lines; and the timing of the corrections is unfavourable first and favourable second. For an investment case that depends on being ahead of the sell-side, the window to be ahead is the buyback, and it opens in August 2027.

11d

Current multiple

B788th of 36Memo topic 11, The fair multiple: the Rater's score1 answer
D-0755
D-07

To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically (on a rolling six-month basis), and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

C+5510th of 35

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. The level of the multiple is industry-driven; the gap to peers and the moves on results days are company-driven; and over the last six months the sector has moved the price far more than the company has. No price-history table was available for this answer (the co-movement file is empty), so the judgement rests on the dated episodes in the first set and the dashboard's peer readings. On that evidence, roughly two-thirds of Carlsberg's six-month price move and most of its multiple's level are explained by the European beverage sector, and about one-third of the move and the discount to peers by the company. For the next 18 months this means two things: the multiple's path depends mainly on whether the defensive rotation and the sector's decade-low rating persist, which the company cannot influence; and the company-driven part, a discount of about 17% to European beverage peers (Deutsche Bank, 29 October 2026) and to AB InBev's 15.6x, can close only on company events (the buyback, China stabilising), which are the same events that decide the earnings cone. The forecasts' predictability is not helped by this: a stock whose multiple is set elsewhere can deliver its earnings and still not deliver its return.

The evidence that the sector sets the level:

  • Where the peers sit. Carlsberg's 24-month-forward P/E of 12.9x is at the 8th percentile of its own ten-year history; Heineken is also at 12.9x and the 7th percentile; Diageo 11.9x at the 0th; Pernod Ricard 10.2x at the 0th; Royal Unibrew 9.9x at the 1st; Campari 17.5x at the 6th; Fevertree 18.2x at the 4th; AG Barr 10.7x at the 4th. Nine of the eleven stocks in the sector are below their own 10th percentile. The de-rating is a sector event (alcohol moderation, weight-loss drugs, Chinese consumption, US tariffs, higher bond yields), not a Carlsberg event. Only AB InBev (15.6x, 46th percentile) and Coca-Cola Europacific Partners (18.6x, 97th) have escaped it, and they are the two with A-rated sell-side momentum or a US listing.
  • The October 2026 rotation. Between 1 and 22 October the brewers rose 9.6-17.4% on defensive flows as the US 10-year yield approached 5.1% and risk appetite fell; Carlsberg rose 14.1% while its average target price fell 0.2% and target-price breadth was -21%. A 14% rise with no estimate support, inside the sector's band, is a sector move by definition.
  • The April 2026 tariff episode. US tariff rhetoric took 2-4 points off the sector on top of a company-specific fall at Carlsberg; the sector component was visible as a common move.
  • Twelve-month returns. Carlsberg +21.8%, Heineken +17.0%, AB InBev +35.5%, Coca-Cola HBC +33.7%, Coca-Cola Europacific Partners +24.6%, Remy +17.3%, against Royal Unibrew -11.4%, Diageo -1.8%, Pernod Ricard -17.3%, AG Barr -13.9%. The dispersion splits by sub-industry (brewers and bottlers up, spirits down), not by company; Carlsberg sits in the middle of the brewer-bottler group. Its relative-strength percentile against the universe is 59, its technical momentum rating D.

The evidence that the company sets the residual:

  • Results days. Carlsberg fell 3.0% on the Q3 2026 trading statement (29 October) when volume missed (-0.4% against +0.6%) and China fell about 10%; Heineken, reporting a day earlier, rose on volume of +3.9%. The two largest European brewers moved in opposite directions on consecutive days on their own numbers: idiosyncratic.
  • The discount. Carlsberg trades about 17% below the European beverage average on calendar 2026 earnings (Deutsche Bank) and 17% below AB InBev on the dashboard's P/E. The discount has a company explanation: leverage of 3.0x after Britvic, 30% of beer volume in China, a revenue algorithm not met, sell-side momentum D against AB InBev's A, and a buy share of 59% falling. These are the items the first set and the second set identified as the case's weaknesses.
  • The 2024-26 path. The five-year range of 9.95x to 18.83x and the fall from the ten-year median of 16.9x to today's 12.9x coincide with the Russian seizure (2023), the Britvic announcement (July 2024, when buybacks were suspended) and the Chinese downturn (2025-26). Those are company events layered on a sector de-rating; the two-year median of 11.89x is the company's post-Britvic level, and today's 12.9x is at the 78th percentile of the last two years, so the October rotation has already moved Carlsberg to the top of its own post-Britvic range.

How far company-driven. Our reading: the sector explains the multiple's level (12-13x for a European brewer today against 16-17x five years ago) and most of the six-month move; the company explains the roughly 15-20% discount within the sector and the results-day moves of 3-5%. A rough attribution of the last six months' +8.4% price return is about +10 points of sector (the October rotation net of the April and September weakness) and about -2 points of company (the Q3 miss, Jefferies' downgrade to Hold on 13 September, DNB Carnegie's trading sell on 9 October). On a rolling six-month basis, therefore, the stock has moved with its peers rather than idiosyncratically, and the idiosyncratic part has been negative.

What this implies for the fair multiple and the 18-month return. First, a re-rating to the guideline 15.27x is a sector call as much as a company call: it needs the European beverage sector to hold its October rating (it gave back 3% in the last week of October) and the company to close its discount. The first set's view that the realistic 18-month route is "8-9% EPS growth plus a buyback at a below-median multiple" rather than a re-rating is consistent with this evidence. Second, the company-driven discount can close by about half if the buyback is announced and China stabilises (AB InBev's precedent: its multiple moved from the bottom of its range as leverage fell below 3x and the USD 6bn buyback started, with the shares up 27-35% in twelve months), and that is worth about 8-10% on the price independent of the sector. Third, the Fitness for Fighting digest's Momentum tilt (Underweight, because defensives have already led) is a sector risk the company cannot hedge: if the 10-year yield falls below 4.5% or risk appetite returns, the brewers give back part of October regardless of Carlsberg's delivery.

For the Consumer. Treat the multiple as about two-thirds exogenous. Set the memo's fair multiple from the company's phase (turning up on the balance sheet, stalled on volume) and the sector's level, not from the ten-year history alone; and present the 18-month return in two parts, the part the company controls (earnings of about +8% a year and dividends of about 3.4%) and the part it does not (the multiple, with a cone of 11-15x).
